.

We will show that the small norm RH problem about
E^{(2)}(z;\xi)
is solvable for large
t
.
By Proposition
6
, the jump matrix
V^{E}
admits the following estimate

\parallel V^{E}(z)-I\parallel_{p}\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\exp\left\{-tK_{p}\right\},&z\in\Sigma_{kj}\setminus U(\xi),\\[6.0pt]
\exp\left\{-tK_{p}^{\prime}\right\},&z\in\Sigma^{\prime}_{kj}.\end{array}\right.

(6.157)

For
z\in\partial U(\xi)\cup\partial\mathbb{B}_{j}
,
M^{(r)}(z)
is bounded, so by Proposition
12
and (
6.149
),

\displaystyle|V^{E}(z)-I|=\big|M^{(r)}(z)^{-1}(M^{lo}(z)-I)M^{(r)}(z)\big|=\mathcal{O}(t^{-1/2}),\text{ }z\in\partial U(\xi)

\displaystyle|V^{E}(z)-I|=\big|M^{(r)}(z)^{-1}(M^{B}_{j}(z)-I)M^{(r)}(z)\big|=\mathcal{O}(t^{-1}),\text{ }z\in\partial\mathbb{B}_{j}.

(6.158)

Therefore, the existence and uniqueness of the RHP
14
can be shown by using a small-norm RH problem
[
22
,
23
]
, moreover its solution can be given by

E^{(2)}(z;\xi)=I+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Sigma^{E}}\dfrac{\left(I+\varpi(s)\right)(V^{E}(s)-I)}{s-z}ds,

(6.159)

where the
\varpi\in L^{\infty}(\Sigma^{E})
is the unique solution of following equation

(1-C_{E})\varpi=C_{E}\left(I\right),

(6.160)

where
C_{E}
is a integral operator defined by

C_{E}(f)(z)=\mathcal{P}^{-}\left(f(V^{E}(z)-I)\right),

(6.161)

and the
\mathcal{P}^{-}
is the usual Cauchy projection operator on
\Sigma^{E}
.
By (
6.158
), we have

\parallel C_{E}\parallel\leq\parallel\mathcal{P}^{-}\parallel\parallel V^{E}(z)-I\parallel_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{O}(t^{-1/2}),

(6.162)

which implies that
1-C_{E}
is invertible for sufficiently large
t
. So
\varpi
exists and is unique,
moreover,

\parallel\varpi\parallel_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma^{E})}\lesssim\dfrac{\parallel C_{E}\parallel}{1-\parallel C_{E}\parallel}\lesssim t^{-1/2},

(6.163)

which gives the existence and uniqueness of
E^{(2)}
.
In order to reconstruct the solution
u(y,t)
of (
1.8
), it is necessary to consider the long time asymptotic behavior of
E^{(2)}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}})
. Note that when we estimate its asymptotic behavior, from (
6.159
) and (
6.157
) we only need to consider the calculation on
\partial U(\xi)
because it approaches zero exponentially on other boundary.

Proposition 15
.

As
z=e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}
, we have

\displaystyle E^{(2)}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}})=I+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Sigma^{E}}\dfrac{\left(I+\varpi(s)\right)(V^{E}-I)}{s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}}ds,

(6.164)

with long time asymptotic behavior

E^{(2)}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}})=I+t^{-1/2}H^{(0)}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho}),

(6.165)

where
H^{(0)}
is explicitly computed by

\displaystyle H^{(0)}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{p(\xi)}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial U_{\xi_{k}}}\dfrac{M^{(r)}(s)^{-1}A_{k}(\xi)M^{(r)}(s)}{(s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}})(s-\xi_{k})}ds+\sum_{k=1}^{p(\xi)}\frac{\omega}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial U_{\xi_{k}}}\dfrac{M^{(r)}(s)^{-1}\Gamma_{3}\overline{A_{k}(\xi)}\Gamma_{3}M^{(r)}(s)}{(s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}})(s-\omega\xi_{k})}ds

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{p(\xi)}\frac{\omega^{2}}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial U_{\xi_{k}}}\dfrac{M^{(r)}(s)^{-1}\Gamma_{2}\overline{A_{k}(\xi)}\Gamma_{2}M^{(r)}(s)}{(s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}})(s-\omega^{2}\xi_{k})}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{p(\xi)}\frac{1}{\xi_{k}-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}}M^{(r)}(\xi_{k})^{-1}A_{k}(\xi)M^{(r)}(\xi_{k})+\sum_{k=1}^{p(\xi)}\frac{\omega}{\omega\xi_{k}-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}}M^{(r)}(\xi_{k})^{-1}\Gamma_{3}\overline{A_{k}(\xi)}\Gamma_{3}M^{(r)}(\xi_{k})

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{p(\xi)}\frac{\omega^{2}}{\omega^{2}\xi_{k}-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}}M^{(r)}(\xi_{k})^{-1}\Gamma_{2}\overline{A_{k}(\xi)}\Gamma_{2}M^{(r)}(\xi_{k}).

(6.166)

The last equality above follows from a residue calculation. Besides, for
j=1,...,6

\displaystyle E^{(2)}(\varkappa_{j})=I+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Sigma^{E}}\dfrac{\left(I+\varpi(s)\right)(V^{E}-I)}{s-\varkappa_{j}}ds,

(6.167)

with long time asymptotic behavior

E^{(2)}(\varkappa_{j})=I+\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho}).

(6.168)

In order to facilitate calculation, denote:

\displaystyle f_{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{n=1}^{3}H^{(0)}_{3n}[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{n3}(e^{\pi i/6})}{[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{33}(e^{\pi i/6})}-\frac{\sum_{n=1}^{3}H^{(0)}_{1n}[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{n1}(e^{\pi i/6})}{[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{11}(e^{\pi i/6})},

(6.169)

and

\displaystyle f_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[\tilde{m}^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{1}\left(\frac{[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{33}(e^{\pi i/6})}{[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{11}(e^{\pi i/6})}\right)^{1/2}\left(T_{1}(e^{\pi i/6})T_{3}(e^{\pi i/6})\right)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sum_{j,n=1}^{3}H^{(0)}_{jn}[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{n1}(e^{\pi i/6})}{[\tilde{m}^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}f_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}[\tilde{m}^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{3}\left(\frac{[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{33}(e^{\pi i/6})}{[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{11}(e^{\pi i/6})}\right)^{-1/2}\left(T_{1}(e^{\pi i/6})T_{3}(e^{\pi i/6})\right)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sum_{j,n=1}^{3}H^{(0)}_{jn}[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{n3}(e^{\pi i/6})}{[\tilde{m}^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{3}}-\frac{1}{2}f_{2}\right).

(6.170)

Finally we consider the error between
E(z;\xi)
and
E^{(2)}(z;\xi)
:

\displaystyle E^{(1)}(z;\xi)=E(z;\xi)E^{(2)}(z;\xi)^{-1},

(6.171)

which is a solution of RH problem only has singularities with:

\displaystyle\lim_{z\to\varkappa_{j}}E^{(1)}(z;\xi)=

\displaystyle\lim_{z\to\varkappa_{j}}M^{R}(z)M^{B}_{j}(\varkappa_{l})^{-1}M^{(r)}(z)^{-1}E^{(2)}(\varkappa_{j};\xi)^{-1}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\frac{E^{(1)}_{-2}(\xi)}{(z-\varkappa_{j})^{2}}+\frac{E^{(1)}_{-1}(\xi)}{z-\varkappa_{j}}+\mathcal{O}(1),

(6.172)

which leads to

\displaystyle E^{(1)}(z;\xi)=I+\sum_{j=1}^{6}\left(\frac{E^{(1),j}_{-2}(\xi)}{(z-\varkappa_{j})^{2}}+\frac{E^{(1),j}_{-1}(\xi)}{z-\varkappa_{j}}\right).

(6.173)

Here, from
M^{R}(z)
and
M^{(r)}(z)
both admit (
4
)-(
4
), there has

\displaystyle E^{(1),j}_{-2}(\xi)=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\alpha^{(2)}_{\pm}&\alpha^{(2)}_{\pm}&\beta^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
-\alpha^{(2)}_{\pm}&-\alpha^{(2)}_{\pm}&-\beta^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right)M^{B}_{j}(\varkappa_{j})^{-1}

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\tilde{\alpha}^{(2)}_{\pm}&\tilde{\alpha}^{(2)}_{\pm}&\tilde{\beta}^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
-\tilde{\alpha}^{(2)}_{\pm}&-\tilde{\alpha}^{(2)}_{\pm}&-\tilde{\beta}^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right)E^{(2)}(\varkappa_{j};\xi)^{-1}.

As
t\to\infty
, (
6.152
) and (
6.168
) lead to that

\displaystyle E^{(1),j}_{-2}(\xi)=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\alpha^{(2)}_{\pm}&\alpha^{(2)}_{\pm}&\beta^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
-\alpha^{(2)}_{\pm}&-\alpha^{(2)}_{\pm}&-\beta^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\tilde{\alpha}^{(2)}_{\pm}&\tilde{\alpha}^{(2)}_{\pm}&\tilde{\beta}^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
-\tilde{\alpha}^{(2)}_{\pm}&-\tilde{\alpha}^{(2)}_{\pm}&-\tilde{\beta}^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right)+\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho}).

(6.186)

Analogously, as
t\to\infty
, the coefficient of
(z-\varkappa_{j})^{-1}
has

\displaystyle E^{(1),j}_{-1}(\xi)=\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho}).

(6.187)

Summarizing above results gives

Proposition 16
.

Taking
z=e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}
, we have

\displaystyle E(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};\xi)=

\displaystyle I+t^{-1/2}H^{(0)}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho}),

(6.188)

where
H^{(0)}
is given by (
6.166
).

7
Contribution from
\bar{\partial}
-components

Now we consider the asymptotics of
M^{(3)}
of the
\bar{\partial}
-problem 4,
whose solution can be given by an integral equation

M^{(3)}(z)=I+\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}\dfrac{M^{(3)}(s)W^{(3)}(s)}{s-z}dm(s),

(7.1)

where
m(s)
is the Lebesgue measure on the
\mathbb{C}
.
Define the left Cauchy-Green integral operator,

fC_{z}(z)=\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}\dfrac{f(s)W^{(3)}(s)}{s-z}dm(s),

then the above equation (
7.1
) can be rewritten as

\left(I-C_{z}\right)M^{(3)}(z)=I.

(7.2)

In the follow, we prove the existence of the operator
\left(I-{C}_{z}\right)^{-1}

in different space-time regions

\xi\in(1,+\infty)\cup(-\infty,-1/8)
and
\xi\in(-1/8,1)
.

7.1
In space-time regions
\xi<-1/8
and
\xi>1

Lemma 8
.

The norm of the integral operator
C_{z}
admits the estimate

\parallel C_{z}\parallel_{L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}}\lesssim t^{-1/2},\ t\to\infty

(7.3)

Proof.

For any
f\in L^{\infty}
,

\displaystyle\parallel fC_{z}\parallel_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\parallel f\parallel_{L^{\infty}}\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{C}\dfrac{|W^{(3)}(s)|}{|z-s|}dm(s).

As
W^{(3)}(s)
is a sectorial function, we just need to consider it on ever sector. We only detail the calculation for matrix function in the sector
\Omega_{1}
as
\xi>1
.

Proposition
16
and
8
imply the boundedness of
M^{(r)}(z)
and
M^{(r)}(z)^{-1}
for
z\in\bar{\Omega}
except a small neighborhood of
\pm 1
,
\pm\omega
and
\pm\omega^{2}
. Take
\Omega_{1}
as an example, then we only need to consider the singularity of
M^{(r)}(z)
at
z=1
. However, (
4.29
) give that near
z=1
,
\bar{\partial}R_{1}(s)\equiv 1
. Hence, we can directly write that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\Omega_{1}}\dfrac{|W^{(3)}(s)|}{|z-s|}dm(s)\lesssim

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{1}\setminus\mathbb{D}(1,\varepsilon)}\dfrac{|\bar{\partial}R_{1}(s)e^{it\theta_{12}}|}{|z-s|}dm(s).

(7.4)

Referring to (
4.28
) in proposition
4
, the right integral can be divided to two part

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{1}\setminus\mathbb{D}(1,\varepsilon)}\dfrac{|\bar{\partial}R_{1}(s)|e^{-t\text{Im}\theta_{12}}}{|z-s|}dm(s)\lesssim I_{1}+I_{2},

(7.5)

with

\displaystyle I_{1}=\iint_{\Omega_{1}\setminus\mathbb{D}(1,\varepsilon)}\dfrac{(|p_{1}^{\prime}(s)|+|\mathcal{X}^{\prime}(|s|)|)e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{|z-s|}dm(s),

\displaystyle I_{2}=\iint_{\Omega_{1}\setminus\mathbb{D}(1,\varepsilon)}\dfrac{|s|^{-1/2}e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{|z-s|}dm(s).

Furthermore, by Corollary
1
, for
z\in\Omega_{1}\setminus\mathbb{D}(1,\varepsilon)
,

e^{-t\text{Im}\theta_{12}}\leq e^{-c(\xi)tv}.

(7.6)

Therefore, via (
6.144
),

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{v}^{\infty}\dfrac{|p_{1}^{\prime}(s)|+|\mathcal{X}^{\prime}(|s|)|}{|z-s|}dudv

\displaystyle\leq\parallel|s-z|^{-1}\parallel_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\left(\parallel p_{1}^{\prime}\parallel_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+\parallel\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\parallel_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\right)e^{-c(\xi)tv}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{+\infty}|v-y|^{-1/2}e^{-c(\xi)tv}dv\lesssim t^{-1/2}.

(7.7)

Before we estimating the second term, we consider for
p>2
,

\displaystyle\left(\int_{v}^{+\infty}|\sqrt{u^{2}+v^{2}}|^{-\frac{p}{2}}du\right)^{{p}/{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{v}^{+\infty}|l|^{-\frac{p}{2}+1}u^{-1}dl\right)^{{p}/{2}}\lesssim v^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}.

(7.8)

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\parallel|s-z|^{-1}\parallel_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\parallel|z|^{-1/2}\parallel_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}e^{-c(\xi)tv}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{+\infty}|v-y|^{1/q-1}v^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}e^{-c(\xi)tv}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{+\infty}v^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-c(\xi)tv}dv\lesssim t^{-1/2}.

(7.9)

So the proof is completed.
∎

Then from (
7.2
), we immediately obtain the existence and uniqueness of
M^{(3)}(z)
for
z\in\mathbb{C}
.
Take
z=e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}
in (
7.1
), then

M^{(3)}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}})=I+\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}\dfrac{M^{(3)}(s)W^{(3)}(s)}{s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}}dm(s).

(7.10)

To reconstruct the solution of (
1.8
), we need following proposition.

Proposition 17
.

There exists a small positive constant
0<\rho<{1}/{4}
and a constant
T_{1}
, such that for all
t>T_{1}
,
M^{(3)}(z)
admits the following estimate

\displaystyle\parallel M^{(3)}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}})-I\parallel=\parallel\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}\dfrac{M^{(3)}(s)W^{(3)}(s)}{s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}}dm(s)\parallel\lesssim t^{-1+\rho}.

(7.11)

Proof.

The proof proceeds along the same steps as the proof of above Proposition. Lemma
8
and (
7.2
) implies that for large
t
,
\parallel M^{(3)}\parallel_{\infty}\lesssim 1
. And for same reason, we only estimate the integral on sector
\Omega_{1}
as
\xi>1
. Let
s=u+vi=le^{i\vartheta}
, then:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\Omega_{1}}\dfrac{|W^{(3)}(s)|}{|s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}|}dm(s)\lesssim

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{1}\setminus\mathbb{D}(1,\varepsilon)}\dfrac{|\bar{\partial}R_{1}(s)e^{it\theta_{12}}|}{|s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}|}dm(s).

(7.12)

For the first term, we use another estimation (
4.37
):

\iint_{\Omega_{1}\setminus\mathbb{D}(1,\varepsilon)}\dfrac{|\bar{\partial}R_{1}(s)e^{it\theta_{12}}|}{|s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}|}dm(s)\lesssim I_{3}+I_{4},

(7.13)

with

\displaystyle I_{3}=\iint_{\Omega_{1}}\dfrac{\left(|p_{1}^{\prime}(s)|+\mathcal{X}^{\prime}(\text{Re}z)\right)e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{|e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}-s|}dm(s),\hskip 14.22636ptI_{4}=\iint_{\Omega_{1}}\dfrac{|s|^{-1}e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{|e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}-s|}dm(s).

(7.14)

For
r\in H^{1,1}(\mathbb{R})
,
r^{\prime}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, which together with
|p_{1}^{\prime}|\lesssim|r^{\prime}|
implies
p_{1}^{\prime}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. therefore,

\displaystyle I_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\left(\parallel p_{1}^{\prime}\parallel_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+\parallel\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\parallel_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\right)\sqrt{2}e^{-c(\xi)tv}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-c(\xi)tv}dv\leq t^{-1}.

(7.15)

The second inequality from
|e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}-s|
is bounded for
s\in\Omega_{1}
. And we recall the sufficiently small positive constant
\rho<\frac{1}{4}
to bound
I_{4}
. And we partition it to two parts:

\displaystyle I_{4}\leq\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\frac{v}{\tan\varphi}}^{+\infty}\dfrac{|s|^{-1}e^{-c(\xi)tv}}{|e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}-s|}dudv+\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{+\infty}\int_{\frac{v}{\tan\varphi}}^{+\infty}\dfrac{|s|^{-1}e^{-c(\xi)tv}}{|e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}-s|}dudv.

(7.16)

Noting that for
0<v<\frac{1}{4}
, we have

|s|^{2}=u^{2}+v^{2}<|s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}|^{2}=(u-{\sqrt{3}}/{2})^{2}+(v-{1}/{2})^{2},

while
v>\frac{1}{4}
,
|s-e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}|^{2}\lesssim|s|^{2}
.
therefore the first integral becomes

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\int_{\frac{v}{\tan\varphi}}^{+\infty}\dfrac{|s|^{-1}e^{-c(\xi)tv}}{|e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}-s|}dudv

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\int_{\frac{v}{\tan\varphi}}^{+\infty}(u^{2}+v^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\rho}{2}}[(u-{\sqrt{3}}/{2})^{2}+(v-{1}/{2})^{2})]^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\rho}{2}}due^{-c(\xi)tv}dv

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\left[\int_{v}^{+\infty}\left(1+\left({u}/{v}\right)^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\rho}{2}}v^{-\rho}d{u}/{v}\right](v-{1}/{2})^{-1+\rho}e^{-c(\xi)tv}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}v^{-\rho}e^{-c(\xi)tv}dv\lesssim t^{-1+\rho}.

(7.17)

The second integral can be bounded in same way

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{+\infty}\int_{\frac{v}{\tan\varphi}}^{+\infty}\dfrac{|s|^{-1}e^{-c(\xi)tv}}{|e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}-s|}dudv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{+\infty}(v-{1}/{2})^{-\rho}e^{-c(\xi)tv}dv\lesssim e^{-\frac{c(\xi)}{2}t}.

(7.18)

This estimate is strong enough to arrive at the result (
7.11
).
∎

7.2
In space-time region
-1/8<\xi<1

Lemma 9
.

The norm of the integral operator
C_{z}
admits the estimate

\parallel C_{z}\parallel_{L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}}\lesssim t^{-1/4},\ \ t\to\infty.

(7.19)

Proof.

For any
f\in L^{\infty}
,

\displaystyle\parallel fC_{z}\parallel_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\parallel f\parallel_{L^{\infty}}\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}\dfrac{|W^{(3)}(s)|}{|z-s|}dm(s).

Like in the case of
\xi\in(-\infty,-1/8)\cup(1,+\infty)
, (
4.98
) gives that the singularity at
\pm 1,\ \pm\omega,\ \pm\omega^{2}
of
M^{R}(z)
will not have influence.

To avoid repetition, we omit the calculation of these regions. And in the proof of this lemma, we focus on the different regions of
\mathbb{C}
. So, we only detail the case for matrix functions having support in the sector
\Omega_{11}
as
\xi\in(-1/8,0)
.

Recall the definition of
W^{(3)}(s)=M^{R}(z)\bar{\partial}R^{(2)}(z)M^{R}(z)^{-1}
.
W^{(3)}(s)\equiv 0
out of
\bar{\Omega}
.
Proposition
16
,
8
and
12
implies the boundedness of
M^{R}(z)
and
M^{R}(z)^{-1}
for
z\in\overline{\Omega_{11}}
, so

\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\Omega_{11}}\dfrac{|W^{(3)}(s)|}{|z-s|}dm(s)\lesssim\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\Omega_{11}}\dfrac{|\bar{\partial}R_{11}(s)e^{2it\theta}|}{|z-s|}dm(s).

(7.20)

Referring to (
4.96
) in proposition
5
, and note that
|\mathcal{X}^{\prime}(\text{Re}z)|=0
in
\overline{\Omega_{11}}
, then the integral
\iint_{\Omega_{11}}\dfrac{|\bar{\partial}R_{11}(s)|}{|z-s|}dm(s)
can be divided into two part:

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{11}}\dfrac{|\bar{\partial}R_{11}(s)|e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{|z-s|}dm(s)\lesssim\hat{I}_{1}+\hat{I}_{2},

(7.21)

with

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{1}=\iint_{\Omega_{11}}\dfrac{|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{|z-s|}dm(s),

(7.22)

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{2}=\iint_{\Omega_{11}}\dfrac{|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{|z-s|}dm(s).

(7.23)

Recall that
z=x+yi
,
s=\xi_{1}+u+vi
with
x,y,u,v\in\mathbb{R}
, then lemma
5
and Corollary
2
give that

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{v}^{R(\xi)}\dfrac{|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|}{|z-s|}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tvu}dudv+\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\dfrac{|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|}{|z-s|}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv

\displaystyle+\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\int_{v}^{+\infty}\dfrac{|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|}{|z-s|}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv.

(7.24)

For the first integral, if the real part of
z
is greater than 0 and less that
\int_{y}^{R(\xi)}
, it is easy to obtain:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{v}^{R(\xi)}\dfrac{|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|}{|z-s|}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tvu}dudv

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\parallel p_{11}^{\prime}\parallel_{2}\parallel|z-s|^{-1}\parallel_{2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}|v-y|^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv

\displaystyle=(\int_{0}^{y}+\int_{y}^{R(\xi)})|v-y|^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv.

(7.25)

And if
y<0
, we do not need to consider the absolute value sign of
|v-y|
, namely,
\int_{0}^{y}
part of the integral is zero. Similarly, if
y>R(\xi)
,
\int_{y}^{R(\xi)}
part of the integral is zero.
We use the inequality
e^{-z}\lesssim z^{-1/4}
in the
\int_{0}^{y}
part:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}(y-v)^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv\lesssim\int_{0}^{y}(y-v)^{-1/2}v^{-1/2}dvt^{-1/4}\lesssim t^{-1/4}.

(7.26)

And for the
\int_{y}^{R(\xi)}
part of the integral, we make the substitution
w=v-y:0\to+\infty

\displaystyle\int_{y}^{R(\xi)}(v-y)^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv=\int_{0}^{+\infty}w^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)ty(w+y)}dw

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}w^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tyw}dwe^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)ty^{2}}\lesssim e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)ty^{2}}.

(7.27)

Similarly, following the same step and using
e^{-z}\lesssim z^{-1/2}
, the second integral in (
7.24
) has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\dfrac{|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|}{|z-s|}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv\leq\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\parallel p_{11}^{\prime}\parallel_{2}\parallel|z-s|^{-1}\parallel_{2}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}|v-y|^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dv\lesssim t^{-1/2}.

(7.28)

And for the last term in (
7.24
), similarly, we have

\displaystyle\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\int_{v}^{R(\xi)}\dfrac{|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|}{|z-s|}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv

\displaystyle\leq\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\parallel p_{11}^{\prime}\parallel_{2}\parallel|z-s|^{-1}\parallel_{2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv}dv\lesssim\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}(v-R(\xi))^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dv

\displaystyle=e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tR(\xi)}\int_{0}^{+\infty}(v-R(\xi))^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)t(v-R(\xi))}d(v-R(\xi))

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tR(\xi)}.

(7.29)

Like the Lemma
8
, we bound
\hat{I}_{2}
. For
p>2
, and
1/p+1/q=1
, analogously, we divide it into three parts:

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{v}^{R(\xi)}\dfrac{|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}}{|z-s|}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tvu}dudv+\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\dfrac{|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}}{|z-s|}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv

\displaystyle+\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\int_{v}^{+\infty}\dfrac{|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}}{|z-s|}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv.

(7.30)

We assume
0<y<R(\xi)
, then the first term has:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{v}^{R(\xi)}\dfrac{|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}}{|z-s|}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tvu}dudv

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\parallel|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}\parallel_{p}\parallel|z-s|^{-1}\parallel_{q}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}v^{1/p-1/2}|y-v|^{1/q-1}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv

\displaystyle=(\int_{0}^{y}+\int_{y}^{R(\xi)})v^{1/p-1/2}|y-v|^{1/q-1}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv.

(7.31)

If
y<0
,
\int_{0}^{y}
part of the integral is zero. And if
y>R(\xi)
,
\int_{y}^{R(\xi)}
part of the integral is zero. Analogously,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}v^{1/p-1/2}|y-v|^{1/q-1}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{y}v^{1/p-1}(y-v)^{1/q-1}dvt^{-1/4}\lesssim t^{-1/4},

(7.32)

and

\displaystyle\int_{y}^{R(\xi)}v^{1/p-1/2}|y-v|^{1/q-1}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv

\displaystyle\leq\int_{y}^{+\infty}(v-y)^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)ty(v-y)}dve^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)ty^{2}}\lesssim e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)ty^{2}}.

(7.33)

The other parts of (
7.30
) has similar estimation like the case of
\hat{I}_{1}
. Then the result is confirmed.
∎

Then from (
7.2
), we immediately arrive at the existence and uniqueness of
M^{(3)}(z)
for
z\in\mathbb{C}
.
Take
z=e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}}
in (
7.1
), then

M^{(3)}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})=I+\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}\dfrac{M^{(3)}(s)W^{(3)}(s)}{s-e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}}dm(s).

(7.34)

To reconstruct the solution of (
1.8
), we need following proposition.

Proposition 18
.

There exist constants
T_{1}
, such that for all
t>T_{1}
, the solution
M^{(3)}(z)
of
\bar{\partial}
-problem admits the following estimation:

\displaystyle\parallel M^{(3)}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})-I\parallel=\parallel\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}\dfrac{M^{(3)}(s)W^{(3)}(s)}{s-e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}}dm(s)\parallel\lesssim t^{-3/4}.

(7.35)

Proof.

The proof proceeds along the same steps as the proof of Proposition
17
. Lemma
9
and (
7.2
) imply that for large
t
,
\parallel M^{(3)}\parallel_{\infty}\lesssim 1
. So we just need to bound
\iint_{\mathbb{C}}\dfrac{|W^{(3)}(s)|}{s-e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}}dm(s)
. And we only give the details on
\Omega_{11}
, the integral on other region can be obtained in the same way. Referring to (
4.96
) and (
4.97
) in proposition
5
, and note that
|\mathcal{X}^{\prime}(\text{Re}z)|=0
in
\overline{\Omega_{11}}
, this integral can be divided into four parts

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\Omega_{11}}\dfrac{M^{(3)}(s)W^{(3)}(s)}{s-e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}}dm(s)\lesssim\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\Omega_{11}}\dfrac{R_{11}(s)e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{s-e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}}dm(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{\frac{v}{\tan\varphi}}^{R(\xi)}\dfrac{\bar{\partial}R_{11}(s)e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{s-e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}}dudv+\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\dfrac{\bar{\partial}R_{11}(s)e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{s-e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}}dudv

\displaystyle+\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\int_{\frac{v}{\tan\varphi}}^{+\infty}\dfrac{\bar{\partial}R_{11}(s)e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{s-e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}}dudv\lesssim\hat{I}_{3}+\hat{I}_{41}+\hat{I}_{42}+\hat{I}_{43}.

(7.36)

Here, near
\xi_{1}
we use (
4.96
) and away from
\xi_{1}
we use (
4.97
):

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{3}=\iint_{\Omega_{11}}\dfrac{|p_{11}^{\prime}(s)|e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}}{|e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}-s|}dm(s),

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{41}=\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{\frac{v}{\tan\varphi}}^{R(\xi)}|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}|e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}-s|^{-1}e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}dudv,

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{42}=\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}|e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}-s|^{-1}e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}dudv,

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{43}=\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\int_{\frac{v}{\tan\varphi}}^{+\infty}|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}|e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}-s|^{-1}e^{-2t\text{Im}\theta}dudv.

For
\hat{I}_{3}
,
|e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}-s|^{-1}
has nonzero maximum. Together with lemma
5
and Corollary
2
, it has:

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{v}^{R(\xi)}|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tvu}dudv+\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv

\displaystyle+\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\int_{v}^{+\infty}|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv.

(7.37)

For the first part in the right hand of above inequality

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{v}^{R(\xi)}|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tvu}dudv\leq\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\parallel p_{11}^{\prime}(s)\parallel_{2}\left(\int_{v}^{+\infty}e^{-2c^{(1)}(\xi)tvu}du\right)^{1/2}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-1/2}\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}v^{-1/2}e^{-2c^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv=\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/4}).

And the second term in (
7.37
) has:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv\leq\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\parallel p_{11}^{\prime}(s)\parallel_{1}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv}dv=\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/2}).

(7.38)

Similarly, the last integral in (
7.37
) has

\displaystyle\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\int_{v}^{+\infty}|p_{11}^{\prime}(|s|)|e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv\leq\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\parallel p_{11}^{\prime}(s)\parallel_{1}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tv}dv=\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tR(\xi)}).

(7.39)

So
\hat{I}_{3}\lesssim t^{-3/4}
. And for
\hat{I}_{41}
,
lemma
5
and Corollary
2
also give:

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{41}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{v}^{R(\xi)}|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tvu}dudv.

(7.40)

Similarly we take
2<p<4
, and
1/p+1/q=1
, then the first integral in above inequality has:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{v}^{R(\xi)}|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}e^{-c^{(1)}(\xi)tvu}dudv

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\parallel|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}\parallel_{p}\left(\int_{v}^{+\infty}e^{-qc^{(1)}(\xi)tvu}du\right)^{1/q}dv

\displaystyle=t^{-1/q}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v^{2/p-3/2}e^{-qc^{(1)}(\xi)tv^{2}}dv\lesssim t^{-3/4}.

(7.41)

To bound
\hat{I}_{42}
, we use the fact that
|s-\xi_{1}|^{-1/2}|e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}-s|^{-1}\lesssim u^{-3/2}
in it integral domain:

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{42}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}u^{-3/2}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv\lesssim\int_{0}^{R(\xi)}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dv\lesssim t^{-1}.

(7.42)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\hat{I}_{43}\leq\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}\int_{v}^{+\infty}u^{-3/2}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dudv

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}v^{-3/2}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dv\lesssim\int_{R(\xi)}^{+\infty}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tv}dv\lesssim t^{-1}e^{-c^{(2)}(\xi)tR(\xi)}.

The result is confirmed.
∎

8
Long-time asymptotic behavior

Now we begin to construct the long time asymptotics of the Novikov equation (
1.8
).
Inverting the sequence of transformations (
3.92
), (
4.104
), (
4.136
) and (
5.19
), we have

\displaystyle M(z)=

\displaystyle M^{(3)}(z)E(z;\xi)M^{(r)}(z)R^{(2)}(z)^{-1}T(z)^{-\sigma_{3}}.

(8.1)

To reconstruct the solution
u(x,t)
by using (
2.118
), we take
z=e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}}
. In this case,
R^{(2)}(z)=I
. Further using Propositions
3
and
17
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle M(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})=

\displaystyle M^{(3)}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})E(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}};\xi)M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})^{-1}.

(8.2)

\blacktriangleright
For
\xi\in(-\infty,-1/8)\cup(1,+\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle M(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})=

\displaystyle M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})^{-1}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho}),

(8.3)

Substitute above estimats into (
2.118
) and (
2.119
), and obtain

\displaystyle u(x,t)=u(y(x,t),t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\tilde{m}_{1}(y,t)\left(\frac{M_{33}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}{M_{11}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{m}_{3}(y,t)\left(\frac{M_{33}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}{M_{11}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}\right)^{-1/2}-1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{r}(y(x,t);\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})\left(T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})\right)^{-1/2}+\left(T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})\right)^{-1/2}-1+\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho}),

where

\displaystyle x(y,t)=y+c_{+}(x,t)=y+\frac{1}{2}\ln\frac{M_{33}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}{M_{11}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y+\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(\frac{[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{33}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}{[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{11}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(\frac{T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})}{T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})}\right)+\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{+}^{r}(x,t;\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})+\frac{1}{2}\ln\left[T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})\right]+\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho}),

(8.4)

where
u^{r}(x,t;\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})
,
[\tilde{m}^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{j}
and
c_{+}^{r}(x,t;\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})
shown in Corollary
3
.

\blacktriangleright
For
\xi\in(-1/8,1)
, Propositions
3
,
16
,
18
and Corollary
3
give that

\displaystyle M(z)=

\displaystyle M^{(3)}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})E(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(I+H^{(0)}t^{-1/2}\right)M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})^{-\sigma_{3}}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/4}).

(8.5)

Similarly, substituting above estimation into (
2.118
) and (
2.119
), then we arrive at:

\displaystyle u(x,t)=u(y(x,t),t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\tilde{m}_{1}(y,t)\left(\frac{M_{33}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}{M_{11}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{m}_{3}(y,t)\left(\frac{M_{33}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}{M_{11}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}\right)^{-1/2}-1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{r}(y(x,t);\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})\left[T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})\right]^{-1/2}+\left[T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})\right]^{-1/2}-1

\displaystyle+f_{1}(y(x,t),t)t^{-1/2}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/4}),

(8.6)

and

\displaystyle x(y,t)=y+c_{+}(x,t)=y+\frac{1}{2}\ln\frac{M_{33}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}{M_{11}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y+\frac{1}{2}\ln\left[{T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})}T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\ln\frac{[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{33}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)+\sum_{n=1}^{3}H^{(0)}_{3n}[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{n3}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)t^{-1/2}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/4})}{[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{11}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)+\sum_{n=1}^{3}H^{(0)}_{1n}[M^{(r)}_{\lozenge}]_{n1}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{6}};y,t)t^{-1/2}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/4})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{+}^{r}(x,t;\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})+\frac{1}{2}\ln\left[{T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})}T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})^{-1}\right]+f_{2}(y(x,t),t)t^{-1/2}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/4}).

(8.7)

Summering obtained results above, we achieve our main results

Theorem 1
.

Let
u(x,t)
be the solution for the Novikov equation (
1.8
) with generic initial data
u_{0}(x)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
and
its associated with scatting data
\left\{r(z),\left\{\zeta_{n},c_{n}\right\}^{6N_{0}}_{n=1}\right\}
. Let
\xi=\frac{y}{t}
and

q^{r}_{\lozenge}(x,t)
be the
\mathcal{N}(\lozenge)
-soliton solution corresponding to scattering data

\tilde{\mathcal{D}_{\lozenge}}=\left\{0,\left\{\zeta_{n},C_{n}T^{2}(\zeta_{n})\right\}_{n\in\lozenge}\right\}
shown in Corollary
3
. Then there exists a large constant
T_{1}=T_{1}(\xi)
such that for all
T_{1}<t\to\infty
, we have

(i)

In space-time regions
\xi<-1/8
and
\xi>1
,

\displaystyle u(x,t)=

\displaystyle u^{r}(y(x,t);\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})\left[T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})\right]^{-1/2}

\displaystyle+\left[T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})\right]^{-1/2}-1+\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho}),\ 0<\rho<{1}/{4},

(8.8)

where

\displaystyle x(y,t)=c_{+}^{r}(x,t;\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})+\frac{1}{2}\ln\left[{T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})}T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})^{-1}\right]+\mathcal{O}(t^{-1+\rho}),

and
u^{r}(x,t;\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})
,
c_{+}^{r}(x,t;\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})
are given by Prop.
3
and Corol.
3
respectively.

(ii)

In space-time region
-1/8<\xi<1
,

\displaystyle u(x,t)=

\displaystyle u^{r}(y(x,t);\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})\left[T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})\right]^{-1/2}

\displaystyle+\left[T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})\right]^{-1/2}-1+f_{1}t^{-1/2}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/4}),

(8.9)

where

\displaystyle x(y,t)

\displaystyle=c_{+}^{r}(x,t;\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})+\frac{1}{2}\ln\left[{T_{1}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})}T_{3}(e^{\frac{\pi i}{6}})^{-1}\right]+f_{2}t^{-1/2}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/4}),

and
u^{r}(x,t;\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})
,
c_{+}^{r}(x,t;\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\lozenge})
are given by Prop.
3
, Corol.
3
, respectively;
and
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are given by (

), respectively.

Our results also show that the poles on curve soliton solutions of Novikov equation has dominant contribution to the solution as
t\to\infty
.
